- Prior exposure to AZD9833 (camizestrant), other investigational SERDs/endocrine agents or
- fulvestrant. Key STEP 2 inclusion criteria · Minumum body weight of 35 kg · Patients who have ESR1m positive disease irrespective of concurrent mutation(s) detected by ctDNA during screening or surveillance. Qualifying ESR1m status will be assessed by a prespecified Sponsor-selected assay at a central laboratory. · Confirmation that the patients are currently on treatment and have received ≥ 6 months of AI (letrozole or anastrozole) + CDK4/6 inhibitor (palbociclib, abemaciclib, or ribociclib) ± LHRH agonist treatment as their initial endocrine based treatment for their advanced disease in accordance with local CDK4/6 inhibitor label or treatment guidelines · Documented absence of disease progression 6 (by investigator assessment) prior to randomisation. · Whenever feasible and available, patients must be willing to provide archival radiological images (CT, MRI or bone scan, if not available, then available PET-CT scan is acceptable) used for tumour assessment at the initiation and/or during CDK4/6 inhibitor + AI for their metastatic disease treatment that support the non-PD assessment. · Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group performance status of 0 to 1. · Patients must have at least one evaluable lesion (defined as one lesion non previously irradiated, measurable and/or non-measurable, that can be accurately assessed at baseline by CT or MRI and is suitable for repeated assessment). Patients with bone disease only must have at least one non previously irradiated lytic or mixed (lytic + sclerotic) bone lesion that can be assessed by CT or MRI; patients with sclerotic/osteoblastic bone lesions only in the absence of evaluable disease are not eligible. Bone scan at baseline is required. · Adequate organ and marrow function · Willingness and ability to provide an archived tissue tumour sample, if available, (FFPE blocks [preferred] or unstained slides) to assess the correlation between genes, proteins, and RNAs relevant to the ER pathways and sensitivity/resistance to the investigational agents' status/expression prior to enrolment. Tissue from the most recent biopsy in the metastatic setting is preferred. If not available (for example bone only disease patients), then archival samples from the primary breast cancer could be used.

## Key STEP 2 Exclusion criteria

- No evidence of disease, or bone only disease with sclerotic/osteoblastic bone lesions only at the screening for STEP 2.

6 (a) Clinical progression defined as unequivocal evidence of disease progression on clinical grounds in the absence of radiological progression. OR (b) Radiological progression defined as unequivocal progression compared to previous imaging obtained during or prior to initiation of CDK4/6 inhibitor + AI.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Have advanced, symptomatic, visceral spread, that are at risk of life-threatening complications in the short term (including patients with massive uncontrolled effusions [pleural, pericardial, peritoneal]), and/or pulmonary lymphangitis.
- Known active uncontrolled or symptomatic CNS metastases, carcinomatous meningitis, or leptomeningeal disease as indicated by clinical symptoms, cerebral oedema, and/or progressive growth. Patients with a history of brain or other CNS metastases or cord compression are eligible if they have been definitively treated with local therapy (eg, radiotherapy, stereotactic surgery) and are clinically stable off anticonvulsants and steroids for at least 4 weeks before the screening phase of the study.
- Any evidence of severe or uncontrolled systemic diseases which, in the investigator's opinion, makes it undesirable for the patient to participate in the study or that would jeopardise compliance with the protocol.
- Chronic gastrointestinal disease, inability to swallow the formulated product, or previous significant bowel resection that would preclude adequate absorption, distribution, metabolism, or excretion of CDK4/6 inhibitor and/or study (AI or AZD9833 (camizestrant)) treatment.
- Persistent non-haematological toxicities (CTCAE Grade &gt; 2) caused by CDK4/6 inhibitor and/or AI treatment. Patients with irreversible toxicity that is not reasonably expected to be exacerbated by study treatment in the opinion of the investigator may be included (eg, hearing loss).
- Patients with CDK4/6 inhibitor treatment-induced symptomatic interstitial lung disease (Grade ≥ 2).
- Known active infection including tuberculosis (clinical evaluation that includes clinical history, physical examination and radiographic findings, and tuberculosis testing in line with local practice), HBV (known positive HBsAg result), and HCV.
- Known to have tested positive for HIV. Patients with HIV may be enrolled if they fulfil the criteria recommended by FDA and ASCO guidelines.
- Second- and third-degree heart block, or clinically significant sinus pause or sinoatrial block. Patients with pacemakers or medically controlled atrial fibrillation are not excluded.
- Left ventricular ejection fraction &lt; 50% with heart failure NYHA Grade ≥ 2.
- Experience of any of the following procedures or conditions in the preceding 6 months: coronary artery bypass graft, angioplasty, vascular stent, any other structural heart disease interventions (eg, cardiac valve repair or replacement surgery or transcatheter valve intervention), severe aortic regurgitation (Grade 3 and 4), myocardial infarction, unstable angina pectoris, cerebrovascular accident, or transient ischaemic attack.
- Untreated electrolyte abnormalities with potential QT-prolonging effect including serum/plasma potassium*, magnesium* and calcium* below the LLN. *Correction of electrolyte abnormalities to within normal ranges can be performed during screening.
- Mean resting QTcF interval &gt; 480 ms (≥ 450 ms for the ribociclib combination), obtained from triplicate ECGs performed at screening.
- Resting heart rate &lt; 55 beats per minute. Repeat measurements are permitted during the screening period.
- Uncontrolled hypertension. Blood pressure systolic &gt; 160 and diastolic &gt; 90 mmHg. Hypertensive patients may be eligible, but blood pressure must be adequately controlled at baseline. Patients may be rescreened regarding blood pressure requirement.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Any concurrent anticancer treatment not specified in the protocol. Concurrent use of nontopical hormonal therapy for non cancer-related conditions (eg, hormone replacement therapy) is not allowed.
- Palliative radiotherapy with a limited field of radiation within 2 weeks or with wide field of radiation or to more than 30% of the bone marrow within 4 weeks before the first dose of study treatment.
- Major surgical procedure (excluding placement of vascular access) or significant traumatic injury within 14 days of the first dose of study treatment or an anticipated need for major surgery and/or any surgery requiring general anaesthesia during the study.

## 5.3.2.1.3. Objectives and estimands

## Primary objective

The primary objective of the study was to demonstrate the superiority of camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor over AI (anastrozole or letrozole) plus CDK4/6 inhibitor by assessment of progression-free survival (PFS). The null hypothesis for the primary time to event endpoint (PFS) was that there is no difference between camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor and AI (anastrozole or letrozole) plus CDK4/6 inhibitor in the probability of a progression event in the FAS (full analysis set) at any time point.

## Estimand for the primary objective

Estimand in plain words: Whether first progression (based on RECIST v1.1) or death, estimated by the population summary measure HR, is prolonged in patients with ESR1 mutation receiving camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor versus AI (anastrozole or letrozole) + CDK4/6 inhibitor in patients with ESR1 mutation regardless of discontinuation or changes in received treatment prior to progression.

| Objectives                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Estimand description/Endpoints                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Primary                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Primary                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| To demonstrate superiority of camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor relative to AI plus CDK4/6 inhibitor by assessment of PFS in the FAS (patients randomised after entering STEP 2 due to detection of ESRIm positivity without concurrent PD). | PFS is defined as the time from randomisation until progression per RECIST 1.1 as assessed by the investigator at the local site, or death due to any cause. The analysis will include all randomised patients, as randomised, regardless of whether the patient withdraws from randomised therapy, receives another anti-cancer therapy, or clinically progresses prior to RECIST 1.1 progression. The measure of interest is the hazard ratio of PFS. |

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on primary estimands

Analysis sets were defined as Enrolled Set (all patients for whom it was recorded on the eCRF that they met all eligibility criteria for STEP 1 [ESR1m Detection Phase]), FAS (all patients who were randomised in the study [after entering STEP 2 due to ESR1m positivity without concurrent progressive disease]). The FAS was used for all the efficacy analyses. Treatment arms were compared on the basis of randomised study treatment, regardless of the study treatment actually received. Patients who were randomised but did not subsequently receive study treatment were included in the analysis in the treatment arm to which they were randomised. The analysis of data using the FAS followed the principles of ITT.

Standard survival analysis methods (stratified Cox, Kaplan Meier) were used for analysing PFS.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Null hypothesis was tested by a stratified log-rank test.

Sensitivity and supplemental analyses: evaluation-time bias, deviation bias, censoring subjects after new anti-cancer therapy in absence of progression or death [for both primary analyses and blinded central review (BICR)], accounting for COVID-19 deaths) and sensitivity (stratification according to eCRF and confounding bias) analyses were also performed to assess the robustness of the primary endpoint.

## Secondary objectives

To demonstrate superiority of camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor relative to AI plus CDK4/6 inhibitor by assessment of PFS2 and overall survival (OS). These are the key-secondary endpoints.

## Estimands for the key-secondary objectives

## Estimand in plain words (PFS2):

Whether second progression (PFS2, investigtor based) following subsequent therapy is prolonged (measured by HR) in patients with ESR1 mutation receiving camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor versus AI (anastrozole or letrozole) + CDK4/6 inhibitor, regardless of missed visits, discontinuation or changes in received treatment prior to first or second progression.

## Estimand in plain words (OS):

Whether overall survival (OS) is prolonged (measured by HR) in patients with ESR1 mutation receiving camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor versus AI (anastrozole or letrozole) + CDK4/6 inhibitor, regardless treatment withdrawal or change of anti-cancer therapy.

| Secondary                                                                                                                               | Secondary                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| To demonstrate superiority of camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor relative to AI plus CDK4/6 inhibitor by assessment of PFS2 in the FAS. | PFS2 is defined as the time from the randomisation to the earliest of the progression event (following the initial progression), subsequent to first subsequent therapy or death. The investigator’s assessment of the date of second progression will be defined according to local standard clinical practice (which may be clinical or radiological progression). This is the primary measure of PFS2. The comparison will include all randomised patients, as randomised, regardless of whether the patient withdraws from subsequent therapy and regardless of missed visits. In a supplementary analysis, PFS2 will be assessed using RECIST 1.1-defined progression as assessed by the investigator at the local site. The measure of interest is the hazard ratio of PFS2. |
| To demonstrate superiority of camizestrant plus CDK4/6 inhibitor relative to AI plus CDK4/6 inhibitor by assessment of OS in the FAS.   | OS defined as the time from randomisation until the date of death due to any cause. The comparison will include all randomised patients, as randomised, regardless of whether the patient withdraws from therapy or receives another anti-cancer therapy. The measure of interest is the hazard ratio of OS; difference in OS probabilities at 2 years and 3 years will be presented.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on the secondary estimands

PFS2 and OS are analysed the same way as primary endpoint, PFS, i.e. by standard methods of survival analysis.

Sensitivity and supplemental analyses for PFS2: evaluation-time bias, Covid-19 and investigator assessment based on RECIST v 1.1.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Interim analyses (IA): (This application is based on results from data cut-off 1 (DCO1) where PFS was significant meeting the two-sided significance level at 0.0001 whereas PFS2 failed and OS was not formally tested due to the testing hierarchy. Subsequent planned analyses provided data regarding the investigated superiority of camizestrant with respect to PFS2 and OS, see figure below).

<!-- image -->

IA for PFS (DCO1): The study was designed to have one IA for PFS after approximately 135 events were observed (45% maturity). The DCO date was determined once the target number of PFS events was reached, but enrolment, follow-up time after last patient randomised, and time between IA and final analysis were also considered, and the DCO date was scheduled after sufficient data were collected for all patients.

Primary analysis for PFS (DCO2): The DCO for the primary analysis of PFS (DCO2) is expected to occur when approximately 195 events (65% maturity) have been observed.

Primary analysis for PFS2 (DCO3): The DCO for the primary PFS2 analysis (DCO3) will occur at 158 PFS2 events.

Primary analysis for OS (DCO4): The DCO for the OS final analysis (DCO4) will occur at 165 OS events (55% maturity).

## 5.3.2.1.4. Results

## Participant flow and numbers analysed

SERENA-6 is a global study in which 3325 patients were screened in 264 centres in 23 countries worldwide. A total of 406 patients entered STEP 2, of whom 315 patients were randomised in a 1:1 ratio to receive the study treatment of either camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor (N = 157) or AI + CDK4/6 inhibitor (N = 158). At the time of DCO1, treatment was ongoing for 49.3% of patients in the camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor arm and 28.9% of patients in the AI + CDK4/6 inhibitor arm.

## Deviations from study plan

The original study protocol (version 1.0 dated 25 March 2021) has been amended four times. Substantial changes in the conduct of the study that were implemented by protocol amendment(s) are shown and briefly described in Table 45. Protocol amendment 3, version 4.0 dated 05 April 2024 did not have any substantial changes, hence, it is not included in the table.

HBsAg and HBV DNA were added to the table of laboratory safety variables. • Text updated in all Sections applicable to clarify that archived tissue tumour sample (either \"FFPE blocks [preferred] or unstained slides\") was to be provided if available. • Removal of the censoring rule for patients who receive another anti- cancer therapy prior to RECIST v1.1 progression from PFS analysis. • Inclusion criterion was updated to change organ and marrow function thresholds. • Exclusion criterion updated to state TdP is as defined by CredibleMeds. • Removal of text recommending an alternative to atropine eye drops.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

1. Progression free survival (PFS)

A HR &lt; 1 favours camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor. The p-value is based on the stratified log-rank test, stratified by the same stratification factors used for the HR.

SERENA-6 protocol did not mandate RECIST assessments and defined an assessment interval of 8-12 weeks. Different types of progression contributed to the PFS2 analyses and only a subset of patients who received subsequent treatment were followed with RECIST methods (58/87 in the camizestrant arm and 60/117 in the control arm). Of note, only in 15 patients in each arm the assessment interval of 8-12 weeks was followed with RECIST methods.

A HR &lt; 1 favours camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor.

With regards to subsequent therapies, more patients in the camizestrant arm received subsequent chemotherapy (45.6%) compared to patients in the AI arm (22.9%). There were no patients in the camizestrant arm who received an antibody-drug conjugate (ADC), while 12% did in the AI arm. Subsequent therapies also included endocrine-based therapy with CDK4/6i, mTORi, and ET monotherapy.

Global health status/QoL

- A reduction in the risk of deterioration in GHS/QoL was observed in the camizestrant arm compared to the AI (HR 0.53; 95%CI: 0.33, 0.82, with a prolonged median TTD [23.03 months in camizestrant arm vs 6.37 months in AI arm]).
- A reduction in the risk of deterioration in pain was observed in the camizestrant arm compared to the AI arm (HR 0.57; 95%CI: 0.37, 0.86, with a prolonged median TTD [16.56 months in camizestrant arm vs 6.47 months in AI arm]).
- A numerical reduction in the risk of deterioration in role functioning (HR 0.74; 95%CI: 0.49, 1.12), physical functioning (HR 0.77; 95%CI: 0.46, 1.30), breast symptoms (HR 0.60; 95%CI: 0.28, 1.25), and arm symptoms (HR 0.66; 95%CI: 0.32, 1.34) was observed in favour of the camizestrant arm compared to the AI arm.

## 5.3.4. In vitro biomarker test for patient selection for efficacy

## Background to diagnostic testing on SERENA-6

Guardant360 CDx (Guardant Health, Redwood City, CA, USA) was the diagnostic test used in the study. In STEP 1 of the SERENA-6 study, blood samples for ctDNA testing were taken every 2 to 3 months during routine clinical visits and screened for ESR1m, with ctDNA analysis centrally using Guardant360 CDx. If at any point during STEP 1 ESR1m was detected, and there was no evidence of clinical or radiographic disease progression, the patient was then eligible to move into the STEP 2 screening of the study.

Guardant360 CDx is a qualitative next generation sequencing-based in vitro diagnostic device that uses targeted high throughput hybridization-based capture technology for detection of single nucleotide variants (SNVs), insertions and deletions (indels) in 55 genes, copy number amplifications (CNAs) in two (2) genes, and fusions in four (4) genes. Guardant360 CDx utilises circulating cell-free DNA (cfDNA) from plasma of peripheral whole blood collected in Streck Cell-Free DNA Blood Collection Tubes (BCTs).

## Scientific Rationale

Mutations in ESR1 (ESR1m), the gene encoding ERα, are a frequent cause of acquired resistance to AI plus a CDK4/6 inhibitor (Bhave et al 2024, Bidard et al 2022, Cardoso et al 2023, Chaudhary et al 2024), which is considered the 1L standard of care therapy for ER-positive HER2-negative advanced breast cancer (ABC). In the pivotal SERENA-6 trial, an ESR1m test is considered positive, if one or more of the 11 most prevalent ESR1m (E380Q, V422del, S463P, L536H, L536P, L563R, Y537C, Y537D, Y537N, Y537S, D538G) that occur in HR-positive mBC) are detected. These mutations are all gain-of function mutations that occur in the LBD and have been shown to confer constitutive ER activity in the absence of estradiol.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Camizestrant was previously shown to bind ERα containing several of the more common ESR1m (potency for mutant ERα was within 10-fold potency of wild-type ERα) (2.6.2). To expand this analysis and to confirm that camizestrant could inhibit the proliferation of ESR1m expressing cells, CRISPRcas9 mediated genome editing was used to generate a comprehensive panel of clonal, isogenic MCF7 cells expressing 10 of the 11 most common ESR1m (D538G, Y537S, E380Q, Y537N/C/D, L536H/P/R and S463P). The clones generated were either heterozygous or homozygous for each mutation. Due to technical reasons, clones expressing the V422del mutation were unable to be generated using this method.

Cells expressing ESR1m showed enhanced growth in the absence of E2 (17β-estradiol) compared to ESR1wt cells. The extent of E2-independent growth varied between mutations, as reported previously and also across the different clones (see Pharmacology Report ONC9833-013).

Parental MCF7 cells (MCF7 Luc or MCF7 PE) or MCF7 cells harbouring ESR1m were treated with camizestrant for 6 days to determine the anti-proliferative activity of camizestrant against a broad range of ESR1m. Camizestrant inhibited the proliferation of parental MCF7 cells with a mean IC50 of 0.08 nM. The potency of camizestrant in MCF7 cells expressing 10 different ESR1m ranged from 0.15 to 8.71 nM.

## Analytical validation aspects of the biomarker assay

Guardant360 CDx is performed by the Guardant Health Clinical Laboratory. Guardant360 CDx is composed of the following major processes:

- Whole Blood Collection and Shipping
- Plasma Isolation and cfDNA Extraction
- Library Preparation and Enrichment
- DNA Sequencing
- Data Analysis and Reporting

Whole blood is collected in Streck Cell-Free DNA BCTs, which stabilise cfDNA and nucleated blood cells for shipping.

Performance characteristics are detailed in the Guardant360 CDx Technical information, with summary performance characteristics for ESR1m below:

- Analytical Accuracy/Concordance with MassARRAY Comparator Method: PPA: 98%, NPA: 85%
- Limit of Blank (LoB): 0% false positive rate
- Limit of Detection (LoD): 1.1% MAF (5ng input), 0.3% MAF (30ng input)
- Precision: PPA: 95.2% with 5ng input.

The performance characteristics show the robustness of Guardant360 CDx for detecting ESR1m.

## Clinical validation aspects

Guardant Health has received EU IVDR Certification for the Guardant360 CDx Liquid Biopsy for tumour mutation profiling across all solid cancers. This certification allows reporting of all ESR1m variants that are included in the biomarker definition for SERENA-6.

Additionally, clinical validation of the Guardant360 assay was demonstrated in the SERENA-6 study. Randomised patients had detectable ESR1m with ctDNA analysis centrally using Guardant360 CDx. Please refer to the section on clincal efficacy for details.

## Justification for the definition of the selected population

The cut-off is determined by the analytical sensitivity of the assay platform. The cut-off metrics is described in the Technical Information (see above) with ≥ 2 DNA molecules: ≥0.001% MAF.

There is a scientific rationale for ESR1-mutation testing based on the mechanism of action for camizestrant and what is known for a comparable oral SERD Orserdu (elacestrant). It was approved in the EU under the IVDR regulation (IVDR 2017/746) as a companion diagnostic for elacestrant to aid in selection of patients with breast cancer with ESR1 missense mutations in May 2024.

In the pivotal study SERENA-6, 3256 patients were tested with Guardant360 CDx, of which 548 patients had a detectable ESR1m. 12906 samples were tested, with 649 (5.0%) failing to give a valid result. Out of the 2885 patients eligible for STEP 1, 2861 patients were tested with Guardant360 CDx. Of those: 521 patients (18.2%; 521/2861) had detectable ESR1m; 266 patients had detectable ESR1m on the first test, reflecting a prevalence of 9.3% (266/2861) from the first test. D538G was the most prevalent ESR1 variant (48.3% of randomised patients), and the majority of patients (81.6%) had a single ESR1 variant.

The clinical data support the utility of using the Guardant360 CDx in selecting patients with a detectable ESR1-mutation.

## 5.3.5. Supportive studies

## SERENA-1 Study

SERENA-1 is a first-in-human (FIH) study evaluating camizestrant as a monotherapy (Parts A and B) and in combination (Parts C through L) in pre- or post-menopausal women with advanced ER-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer. An overview of the SERENA-1 study is presented in Table 20. SERENA-1 enrolled patients with metastatic or locoregionally recurrent disease refractory or intolerant to existing standard therapies. Majority of patients had received prior CDK4/6 inhibitor.

## Camizestrant (75 mg) in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor

Patients received a median of 2 to 3 prior regimens in the advanced setting, including 0 to 2 prior chemotherapy regimens and a median of 1 to 2 ETs. Patients treated with camizestrant (75 mg) in combination with palbociclib, abemaciclib, and ribociclib were generally heavily pre-treated (Table 64).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Despite extensive prior lines of therapy, camizestrant in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor exhibited clinical activity in patients with ER-positive ABC.

## SERENA-2 Study

SERENA-2 is an open-label, randomised Phase II study evaluating the efficacy and safety of camizestrant (75, 150, and 300 mg, oral QD) as a monotherapy in comparison with fulvestrant administered according to its label in post-menopausal women with advanced ER-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer. Prior chemotherapy and/or ET was permitted, albeit with no more than one line of chemotherapy and one line of ET for advanced disease. Prior treatment with CDK4/6-inhibitor was permitted.

In total, 240 patients were randomised and received treatment. The age distribution was as expected and well balanced across treatment arms with approximately half of the patients (118 [49.2%] patients) in the ≥ 50 to &lt; 65 years old age group. The majority of patients were White (226 [94.2%] patients). By design, half of the patients (119 [49.6%] patients) reported prior use of CDK4/6 inhibitors. The majority of these ABC patients had metastatic disease (224 [93.3%] patients). At least 1 ESR1m was detectable in 88 (36.7%) patients at study entry, with a slightly higher

proportion observed in the fulvestrant treatment arm (35/73 [47.9%] patients).

During study conduct, review of the SERENA-1 Phase I camizestrant monotherapy data revealed no increment in efficacy with increases of dose above 150 mg, in terms of ORR, CBR at 24 weeks, and PFS. Based on this information, the Sponsor closed further recruitment to the 300 mg arm of SERENA-2 on 21 December 2020. Data accrued from the camizestrant 300 mg treatment arm were summarised and reported in the CSR as appropriate. See also 5.3.1.

## Primary Endpoint - PFS

At the primary analysis (DCO date of 30 August 2022), camizestrant at both 75 mg and 150 mg showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS over fulvestrant (HR = 0.59; 90% CI: 0.42, 0.82; p = 0.0167 for the 75 mg dose arm and HR = 0.64; 90% CI: 0.46, 0.89; p = 0.0090 for the 150 mg dose arm) (Table 65). Median PFS by investigator assessment was 7.2 months (90% CI: 3.7, 10.9) in the camizestrant 75 mg arm, 7.7 months (90% CI: 5.5, 12.9) in the camizestrant 150 mg arm, and 3.7 months (90% CI: 2.0, 6.0) in the fulvestrant arm (Table 65).

## 5.3.8. Patient experience data (PED)

As part of the study design process, consultations were held with 3 groups of breast cancer patients with clinical trial experience, as part of the Sponsor's Patient Partnership Program. Patients were provided with study background details, and draft patient communication materials (eg, ICF). Consultation was in the form of 1.5 hour meetings (one with each group of 3 to 4 patients), and written feedback provided by patients. The feedback focused on patient-impacting study design elements (eg, visit schedule, sample collection, study activities (eg, CT scans), storage and use of data, ICF process and wording, etc,). Patient feedback was very positive regarding the study concept of monitoring disease in order to switch treatment, and to further understand the disease response to treatment.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

This feedback provided by patients was used to optimise the study from a patient-centricity perspective, and to edit and develop the patient communication material.

In addition, see Figure 51: Kaplan-Meier Plot for Time to Deterioration (months) in GHS/QoL for EORTC QLQ-C30 (Full Analysis Set) in section 5.3.2.1.4. Results for patient reported outocomes (PROs) assessment in SERENA-6.

## 5.3.10. Overall discussion and conclusions on clinical efficacy

## 5.3.10.1. Discussion

## Design and conduct of the clinical studies

The approval is primarily based on data from the pivotal study, SERENA-6, which is a randomised, phase 3, double-blind, placebo-controlled multicentre study. The design of the pivotal study, SERENA6, has been considered overall acceptable and data from this study support the proposed indication. However, the applied indication will lead to a new treatment paradigm, as patients are switched early based on a biomarker-selection (ESR1-mutation), which is not part of the current standard of care. CHMP scientific advice underlined that from a clinical perspective the need for support from PFS2 data of high quality and OS data would be considered key.

Patients eligible for inclusion had advanced ER-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer, and were treated with an AI + CDK4/6 inhibitor in the first-line advanced setting.

During the evaluation, it was clarified that SERENA-6 allowed patients with bone-only evaluable disease (regardless of measurability). Moreover, patients with evaluable disease included both patients with measurable and non-measurable disease that can be assessed by CT or MRI in line with RECIST 1.1 guidelines. Those patients with bone disease only, in most cases, have a lytic component that can be distinguished in CT or MRI and were not excluded from the SERENA-6 study. This resulted in exclusion of 14 patients out of 406 patients (3.4%) during STEP2 screening and this low fraction of exclusions is considered reassuring, and it is agreed that this should not have any impact on the indication, since no different efficacy is expected based on presence of measurable (bone) disease or not.

Screening of 3050 patients led to randomisation of 315 patients in a 1:1 ratio, and the randomisation was stratified by time from initiation of CDK4/6 inhibitor + AI to randomisation (&lt;18 months vs ≥18 months); ESR1m detectable at first ctDNA test; visceral vs non-visceral disease; and CDK4/6 inhibitor used (i.e. palbociclib vs abemaciclib vs ribociclib). Most of the stratification factors were endorsed as they were considered to be prognostic factors (e.g. visceral disease; early detection of ESR1-mutation indicating treatment resistance; and time on 1L therapy). However, type of CDK4/6i used may not be prognostic, but stratification can support relevant subgroup analyses, if sufficiently powered. It should be noted that in previous advice (EMA/SA/0000051172, dated April 22, 2021) it was questioned whether presence of ESR1m (first versus subsequent) was an adequate way to capture heterogeneity in the study population, but in this assessment the Applicant's choice of stratification factors has been considered acceptable. During the procedure, it was clarified that among the 91 enrolled patients with ESR1m, who were not subsequently randomised, 82.8% (n=83) were classified as \"screen failure\" or \"failure to meet randomisation criteria\". Most commonly, this was due to clinical or radiographic progression: 52.7% (n=48); bone-only disease measurability: 15.4% (n=14); and Organ/marrow function: 8.8% (n=8). The reasons were noted and it was considered expected that some patients would be excluded due to non-measurable disease and organ/marrow dysfunction. However, the exclusion of 48 patients due to disease progression was considered substantial, and for this reason this aspect has been included in the revised wording of indication, clarifying that 'no progression' should be evident at the time of treatment switch to camizestrant. Crossover within the study was not permitted and all randomised patients continued study treatment until objective radiological disease progression as defined by RECIST v1.1, unacceptable toxicity occurred, treatment discontinuation criterion was met, the patient withdrew consent, or death. This was considered acceptable. The primary objective of the pivotal study SERENA-6 was showing superiority in PFS by Investigator of camizestrant + CDK4/6i over SoC (AI + CDK4/6i), which was considered a clinically relevant and acceptable primary endpoint in the studied first-line setting of advanced ER+, HER2-negative breast cancer. In the context of a double-blind, randomised placebo-controlled pivotal study, it was considered fully acceptable to have investigator-assessed PFS as the primary endpoint. PFS by BICR and assessment of early discrepancy rate (EDR) and late discrepancy rate (LDR) have been considered as indicators of quality. Secondary objectives of key relevance were PFS2 and OS. Other secondary endpoints were time to first subsequent therapy (TFST), time to second subsequent therapy (TSST), and chemotherapy-free interval. There were four amendments to the initial protocol and none of the changes have been considered to have had any major impact on the study results.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Efficacy data and additional analyses

The clinical DCO for this submission of the first interim analysis of SERENA-6 was 28 November 2024 and the median duration of follow-up was 13.1 months in the camizestrant arm and 12.1 months in the control (AI) arm. During the procedure, the Applicant provided updated data from DCO2 (30 June 2025) and further on from DCO3 (02 January 2026). Baseline data showed that the median age was ~61 years, 63% of the patients were less than 65 years of age, and 37% of the patients were ≥65 years in the study population, which was considered reflective of the targeted patient population. Three male patients were included, all in the control arm, which was considered acceptable, since breast cancer is rare in men. Still, the results from the pivotal trial were considered extrapolatable to men with ER-positive, HER2-negative metastatic breast cancer in line with previous EMA decisions (see Orserdu (elacestrant) EPAR, Inluriyo (imlunestrant EPAR)), based on the common biological and pharmacological rationale. The majority of patients were either White (63.2%) or Asian (23.2%), which was equally distributed between both arms and the fraction of EU-like patients is considered acceptable for interpretation of the study results in an EU context. Less than half of the included patients had visceral metastases (43.5%) versus non-visceral disease (56.5%), which was a stratification factor and equally distributed between the treatment arms. Patients were generally of good ECOG Performance status (PS), and either of ECOC PS 0 (68.2% vs 62.0%, respectively) or ECOG PS 1 (30.6% vs 35.4%, respectively); which was skewed a bit in favour of the camizestrant arm. All approved CDK4/6 inhibitors were allowed in the pivotal SERENA-6 study, and approximately 75.0% of patients were randomised to palbociclib, compared with ~15.0% to ribociclib and ~10.0% to abemaciclib. While this distribution was not optimal, it is attributable to the initial study design, as CDK4/6 inhibitors other than palbociclib were permitted only later during the recruitment period following a protocol amendment. It was noted that more than half of the patients had a detectable ESR1 mutation at the first ctDNA test, having in mind that patients were treated for at least 6 months with an AI + CDK4/6i before the screening was initiated, while 46.7% had an ESR1-mutation detected at the subsequent ctDNA test. This translated into approximately one third (37.5%) with less than 18 months duration of first-line SoC treatment with an AI + CDK4/6 before the ESR1 mutation was detected indicating treatment resistance; while two-thirds had longer time on first-line treatment (&gt;18 months) before an ESR1 mutation were detected. These numbers were considered in line with the known median PFS for AI + palbociclib of 27.6 months in the PALOMA-2 study (R.Finn et al., 2016). Prior adjuvant CDK4/6i was allowed but was only given to a very small subset of the included patients, i.e. 3.8% (6/157 patients) and 7% (11/158 patients) of the patients in each arm, respectively. This is acceptable, in line with SoC treatment in the early breast cancer setting and is not considered to have had an impact on the study results. At the time of DCO1, 45.2% versus 63.3% events were observed, respectively, and the primary endpoint of PFS by Investigator was improved from a median of 9.23 months in the AI arm to 15.97 months in the camizestrant arm. The difference of 6.74 months was statistically significant with a HR of 0.44 (95%CI: 0.31; 0.60). The KM curves separated early and stay separated, which was considered promising. At DCO2, mature PFS data were provided with 57.3% and 72.8% events in the camizestrant arm vs the control arm, respectively and a HR of 0.46 (95% CI: 0.34, 0.62), consistent with DCO1. At DCO3, there was 63.1% and 78.5% of PFS events in the camizestrant arm vs the control arm, respectively and a HR of 0.45 (95% CI: 0.34, 0.59), consistent with DCO1 and 2. The median PFS by INV was 16.76 months in the camizestrant arm vs 9.23 months in the control arm, resulting in 7.53 months of improvement. The KM curves are clearly separating after 3 months of treatment. PFS by BICR at DCO2 indicated 38.2% and 47.5% events in the camizestrant arm vs the control arm, respectively, HR 0.46 (95% CI: 0.32, 0.66). The median PFS by BICR was 18.43 months in the camizestrant arm vs 12.68 months in the control arm, resulting in 5.75 months of improvement (at DCO1 it was 7.75 months). Although the point estimate for HR of PFS by BICR (HR 0.43, 95%CI: 0.29, 0.63) was similar to that of PFS by Investigator (0.46), the median PFS by BICR was estimated to be longer according to BICR compared to by Investigator in the camizestrant arm (18.43 versus 16.59 months), while median PFS by BICR was also longer in the AI arm compared to by Investigator (12.68 vs 9.23 months). It was noted that the updated concordance between the investigator assessment and BICR was still poor, especially regarding RECIST progression disagreements, as there were less PD cases called in the camizestrant arm. However, since PFS by INV was the primary endpoint, this observation is not considered to have had a major impact on the interpretation of the study results. PFS2 data at DCO2 in the FAS were reported to be 25.75 months with camizestrant compared to 19.35 months in the control arm, HR 0.56 (95%CI: 0.39; 0.80). At DCO3 (time of the planned primary analysis of PFS2), a statistically significant result was reported with a HR of 0.63; 95%CI: 0.46, 0.86; p-value 0.00373). Median PFS2 was 25.72 months in the camizestrant arm compared to 19.06 months in the control arm. This PFS2 result cannot be considered supportive due to the poor quality of the collected PFS2 data. Consequently, the approval was considered supported only by the positive outcome on PFS and the consistent result of updated PFS by BICR. The SERENA-6 protocol did not mandate specific post-study treatments but recommended to follow ESMO and NCCN guidelines. Results show a high level of heterogeneity in subsequent treatment choices, partly not reflected in the treatment guidelines. Furthermore, tumor assessments by RECIST methods were not mandated but an assessment interval of 8-12 weeks was defined. Different types of progression contributed to the PFS2 analyses and only a subset of patients who received subsequent treatment were followed with RECIST methods (58/87 in the camizestrant arm and 60/117 in the control arm). It was clarified that only in 15 patients in each arm the assessment interval of 8-12 weeks was followed with RECIST methods. This poor data quality with large heterogeneity in subsequent treatment choices, heterogeneity in tumor assessments intervals including missing assessments led to consider the PFS2 data unreliable, and an overestimation of the median PFS2 in both arms was considered likely. The extent of the reported difference in PFS2 could not be clarified. Summary of subsequent therapies showed that more patients in the camizestrant arm received subsequent chemotherapy (45.6%) compared to patients in the AI arm (22.9%). There were no patients in the camizestrant arm who received an antibody-drug conjugate (ADC), while 12% did in the AI arm. Subsequent therapies also included endocrine-based therapy with CDK4/6i, mTORi, and ET monotherapy. Since the pivotal study was testing whether an early switch of ET backbone therapy in combination with the same CDK4/6i at the time of detection of an ESR1 mutation in ctDNA was better than awaiting radiological disease progression (PD) or even clinical progressive disease, for any PFS benefit to be considered clinically relevant, it must be supported by benefit in longer term time-to-event endpoints and at least no OS detriment. The new treatment paradigm can be considered as early second-line therapy, and entails multiple testings of the blood for ESR1 mutations, which can be challenging for the patients emotionally, since the detection of an ESR1 mutation is associated with treatmentresistance. On the other hand, it has been considered clinically meaningful to prolong time on first-line endocrine based therapy, since radiological and/or clinical progression is associated with symptoms that may be severe, including treatment-resistant pain as well as potentially severe disabilities and disease manifestations such as spinal cord compression resulting in paralysis and liver failure. In this context, it has been considered clinically relevant to detect an early sign of treatment-resistance and upcoming treatment failure and avoid the first progression on first-line therapy to be symptomatic. Therefore, the clinical study design of SERENA-6 has been considered to adequately answer the question on whether a switch to camizestrant from an AI backbone in combination with CDK 4/6i was really beneficial for patients, since the patients were randomised in a blinded and a placebo-controlled study at the time of detection of the known resistance mutation of ESR1 in ctDNA and because a clinically relevant PFS prolongation has been observed in the camizestrant arm using this approach. OS has further matured at DCO3 with ~30% events in both arms, HR: 0.87 (95% CI: 0.57, 1.30), which is an improvement of the HR from DCO1 and 2 (HR 0.91 and HR 0.92, respectively). At this time, there was no apparent detriment on OS but no clear OS benefit either. A post-hoc RMST analysis indicated no apparent detrimental effect on OS. It was considered still early to conclude on these updated OS data. The Applicant will submit the final OS data planned with 55% maturity (165 OS events) as a Post authorisation efficacy study (PAES) in Annex II.D by December 2028. These data are considered key to the B/R balance and will address criteria 2a of the Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) No. 357/2014 (when an initial efficacy assessment that is based on surrogate endpoints, which requires verification of the impact of the intervention on clinical outcome or disease progression or confirmation of previous efficacy assumptions). Other secondary endpoints included Time to First Subsequent Therapy or death (TFST), Time to Second Subsequent Therapy or death (TSST), and Time to Second Subsequent Therapy or death. At DCO2, the PFS benefit was supported by longer TFST and TSST in the camizestrant arm compared to the control arm. Chemotherapy-free survival was also updated at DCO3. The interpretability of the reported difference was limited as the choice of post-study treatments was not prospectively defined. Due to heterogeneity in the choice of type of next-line treatment, more patients in the camizestrant + CDK 4/6i arm received classical chemotherapy and more patients in the control arm received ADCs post-progresssion. A descriptive analysis of the change in ESR1m VAF detected in plasma according to the treatment arm was provided. In the camizestrant arm, levels decline (median change of -100%) while levels rise (median +66.7%) in patients in the AI arm. Although these data are exploratory in nature, the observed decline in the treatment arm and rising ctDNA level in the control arm was considered supportive.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Subgroup analyses of the primary endpoint, PFS by Investigator, were performed regarding disease site; ESR1m-detection (first vs subsequent test); time from initiation of CDK4/6i to randomisation; type of CDK4/6i; and ESR1 variants. Additionally, subgroup analyses of age groups and menopausal status are considered relevant to comment. Disease site (visceral vs non-visceral) subgroup analyses showed clinically relevant benefit in both subgroups with maybe a trend toward better outcomes for the patients with non-visceral disease (HR 0.51 vs HR 0.44 for patients with visceral vs non-visceral); however, this finding may be uncertain due to still few events in the camizestrant arm. Likewise, the benefit seems to be increased in patients with early detected ESR1m (first test) with a HR of 0.36

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

compared to later (HR 0.61); and this finding was considered due to the poorer outcomes for the patients in the control arm, who continued AI + CDK4/6 and was consistent with the knowledge that

SERDs such as camizestrant primarily have shown relevant efficacy in patients with an ESR1 mutation. The updated subgroup analyses from the DCO2 continued to show benefit in favour of camizestrant and no discordant results for PFS in the requested subgroups were identified. Hence, the point estimate for HR was still below 1 across important subgroups including the stratification factors: CDK4/6 inhibitor combination partner (palbociclib, ribociclib, or abemaciclib), ESR1m detectable at first ctDNA test or subsequent ctDNA test, disease site (visceral vs non-visceral), and time from initiation of AI + CDK4/6 inhibitor to randomisation (&lt; 18 months or ≥ 18 months). Results on the subgroups ESR1m detectable at first ctDNA test or subsequent ctDNA test were identified as interesting. For the camizestrant arm, the median PFS was comparable in both subgroups, while in the control arm the median PFS was longer in the subgroup with ESR1m detection at a subsequent test. PFS according to type of CDK4/6i used was not conclusive, as the vast majority of patients (75%) received palbociclib, while very few patients received ribociclib or abemaciclib. This was considered acceptable and the type of CDK4/6i was not considered to be an important effect modifier for camizestrant, when combined. Importantly, the benefit was considered clinically relevant across subgroups of age categories and regarding menopausal status. while the studies supported the proposed dose and continued clinical development, there was no need

Results from the supportive clinical studies SERENA-1 and -2, respectively, were also provided and to have the data reflected in the SmPC. Overall, the benefit of camizestrant was based on the clinical data from the pivotal study SERENA-6.

## 5.3.10.2. Conclusions on the clinical efficacy

Overall, the efficacy assessment concluded that: SERENA-6 was testing a new treatment paradigm, i.e. proactively searching for the earliest timepoint to diagnose an ESR1-mutation in parallel to ongoing first-line treatment of ER+ HER2- mBC with the combination of an aromatase inhibitor (AI) + a CDK4/6 inhibitor, leading to replacement of the AI by camizestrant (still in combination with the CDK4/6i) without evidence of clinical and/or radiological progression. This new approach can be considered an early second-line therapy; however, without experiencing a symptomatic first-line treatment failure, which may include severe disase symptoms, including treatment-resistant pain as well as potentially severe disabilities and disease manifestations such as spinal cord compression resulting in paralysis and liver failure. The benefit of the new treatment paradigm was primarily based on the demonstrated PFS benefit of 7.53 months and the HR of 0.45 (95% CI: 0.34; 0.59) with camizestrant, considering the randomised, blinded and placebo-controlled study design. This positive outcome was supported by a consistent result of updated PFS by BICR which continued to support clinical benefit in favour of the camizestrant arm. Based on the available data and in the absence of an OS detriment, the efficacy of camizestrant has been considered sufficiently established to supports its use in the proposed indication. In order to further characterise the efficacy of camizestrant, the MAH will submit the final OS analysis as a PAES in Annex II.D by December 2028.

## 5.4. Clinical safety

Please refer to the table of studies in section 5.1.2. For the purpose of this document, the following definitions apply: 'Adverse event - AE' means any untoward medical occurrence in a subject to whom a medicinal product is administered and which does not necessarily have a causal relationship with this treatment. 'Serious adverse event - SAE' means any untoward medical occurrence that at any dose requires

inpatient hospitalisation or prolongation of existing hospitalisation, results in persistent or significant disability or incapacity, results in a congenital anomaly or birth defect, is life-threatening, or results in death. The definition (in line with ICH E2A) includes important medical events that may not be immediately life-threatening or result in death or hospitalisation but may jeopardise the patient or may require intervention to prevent one of the other outcomes listed in the definition above.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

'Adverse drug reaction - ADR' means any untoward and unintended response to a medicinal product related to any dose administered, for which, after a thorough assessment, a causal relationship between the medicinal product and the adverse event is at least a reasonable possibility, based for example, on their comparative incidence in clinical trials, or findings from epidemiological studies and/or on an evaluation of causality from individual case reports.

## 5.4.1. Safety data collection

The pivotal analysis of the safety and tolerability data of camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor (palbociclib, ribociclib, or abemaciclib) in the proposed indication is based on the pre-planned IA (DCO1) of data from the Phase III SERENA-6 study (DCO1 date: 28 November 2024). The overall clinical study pooling strategy used to support safety claims for this submission are presented in Table 66. The integrated safety datasets included:

- Camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor combination pool: Combination pool included safety data from studies SERENA-6 and SERENA-1 (Parts C, D, G, H, K, and L) evaluating the same therapeutic combinations (camizestrant 75 mg QD + all CDK4/6 inhibitors, ie, camizestrant + palbociclib, camizestrant + ribociclib, and camizestrant + abemaciclib). Pooled data of camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor combination was also presented by individual CDK4/6 inhibitor (ie, palbociclib, ribociclib, or abemaciclib).
- Monotherapy pool: The monotherapy pool included safety data of camizestrant from studies SERENA-1 (Parts A and B), SERENA-2, Japan and China Phase I studies.

Table 65: Safety Pooling Strategy

|                          | Camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor combination therapy pool                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Camizestrant monotherapy pool                                                                                                                            |
|--------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Eligible subjects        | • Patients who took at least one dose of camizestrant 75 mg QD in combination therapy with either palbociclib, ribociclib, or abemaciclib. • Patients who received at least one dose of either palbociclib, ribociclib, or abemaciclib but not camizestrant were not included | • Patients who took at least one dose of camizestrant 75 mg QD as a monotherapy. • Patients who took other dose levels of camizestrant were not included |
| Treatment group(s)       | • Combined CDK4/6 inhibitors ° cmizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitors (N = 263) • Individual CDK4/6 inhibitors ° camizestrant + palbociclib (N = 141) ° camizestrant + ribociclib (N = 83) ° camizestrant + abemaciclib (N = 39)                                                     | camizestrant (N = 117)                                                                                                                                   |
| Studies to be integrated | SERENA-6 (N = 155) and SERENA-1 (Parts C, D, G, H, K, and L; N = 108)                                                                                                                                                                                                         | SERENA-1 (Parts A and B; N = 24), SERENA-2 (N = 74), Japan Phase I study (N = 7), and China Phase I study (N = 12)                                       |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The analyses of pooled data included pooled analyses of AEs and laboratory data. The pooled data are presented side-by-side with the SERENA-6 safety data to provide comparative descriptive analysis.

In SERENA-6 study, after data unblinding, it was determined that a patient randomised to the AI + CDK4/6 inhibitor arm received treatment with camizestrant instead of with camizestrant-matching placebo for 1 cycle due to a study treatment kit dispensing error. According to the SAP, this patient should have been included in the camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor arm for safety analysis but was analysed in the AI + CDK4/6 inhibitor arm based on the ITT principle.

Of note, data collection methodologies were comparable in the individual studies, which allows for meaningful comparisons of safety variables to be made.

The criteria used in recording of AEs in individual studies pooled for safety are summarised in Table 68.

Table 67: Criteria for AE Assessment

| Criteria   | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| AE         | An AE is the development of any untoward medical occurrence (other than progression of the malignancy under evaluation) in a patient or clinical study patient administered a study treatment and which does not necessarily have a causal relationship with this treatment. An AE can therefore be any unfavourable and unintended sign (eg, an abnormal laboratory finding), symptom (for example nausea, chest pain), or disease temporally associated with the use of a medicinal product, whether or not considered related to the study treatment. |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

| Criteria                        | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|---------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| TEAE reporting period           | The integrated analysis of AEs was based on all TEAEs from all studies. TEAEs for the patients on combination therapy, camizestrant/AI + CDK4/6 inhibitor, were defined as those AEs with onset or that worsen (by investigator report of a change in intensity) after the first dose of camizestrant, AI (anastrozole or letrozole), and CDK4/6 inhibitor (palbociclib, ribociclib or abemaciclib), whichever occurred first, and on or before the date of last dose of camizestrant/anastrozole/letrozole + 28 days. For patients on the camizestrant monotherapy, TEAEs were defined in a similar way but excluding the AI and CDK4/6 inhibitor dose. In SERENA-2 and SERENA-6, TEAEs were further restricted to those that occurred prior to the start of any subsequent cancer therapy. |
| TEAE variables collected        | The following variables were collected for each TEAE: TEAE term (verbatim), TEAE start and stop date, maximum CTCAE grade, TEAE seriousness, investigator causality assessment against the study drug (yes or no), action taken with study drug, and outcome of the TEAE.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Coding of TEAEs                 | The most current version of MedDRA was used for coding of the AE data. Data from studies originally reported in previous versions of MedDRA were up-versioned for integration purposes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Capturing TEAE severity         | In SERENA-1 CTCAE Version 4.0 was used, whereas in China and Japan Phase I studies, SERENA-2, and SERENA-6 studies, CTCAE Version 5.0 was used. The AEs reported in this remained with the original CTCAE grading recorded in the individual studies by the investigator. The difference in CTCAE versions between the studies was not considered to have an impact on the interpretation of the AE summaries.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Causality assessment            | All studies collected study drug relationship as assessed by the investigator with respect to camizestrant. For SERENA-6, it was also collected for CDK4/6 inhibitor and AI. The investigator assessed causal relationship between the study treatment and each AE and answered 'yes' or 'no' to the question 'Do you consider that there is a reasonable possibility that the event may have been caused by the investigational product?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Dosing action taken due to TEAE | All studies reported where camizestrant was discontinued due to an AE. The available action in the treatment management guidelines allowed for \"Dose not changed\", \"Dose reduced\", \"Drug interrupted\", \"Drug permanently discontinued\" and\" Not applicable\" in all studies.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

- b. Category is defined based on CIOMS III convention before rounding: (1) very common (≥ 1/10), (2) common (≥1/100 to &lt; 1/10), (3) uncommon (≥ 1/1,000 to &lt; 1/100), (4) rare (≥ 1/10,000 to &lt; 1/1,000), (5) very rare (&lt; 1/10,000), and (6) NK (not known -cannot be estimated from available data)

Table is sorted in alphabetical order by ADR grouped term and descending frequency for preferred term (camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor from SERENA-6).

Patients with multiple ADRs are counted once for each grouped term/preferred term.

Note: ADRs with an onset date on/after date of first dose; ADRs with onset date prior to dosing which worsen after dosing and ADRs occurring up to 28 days following date of last dose.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A screening process for ADRs was conducted when a summary table of overall frequencies of AEs by treatment group was available for the SERENA-6 study.

The approach to identify the ADRs was as follows:

- 1 All AEs (by PT) reported in at least 10% (or an alternative appropriate threshold chosen) of patients in the camizestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor arm in SERENA-6 with at least 5% difference (or an alternative appropriate threshold) between the 2 treatment arms were considered as potential ADRs and subject to clinical review.
- 2 A similar approach was applied to Grade 3-4 or higher events only, whereby a 2% difference (or an alternative appropriate threshold) was used to screen for potential ADRs.
- 3 Clinical review of all safety data from SERENA-6, SERENA-1, SERENA-2, China and Japan Phase I studies was performed to identify any ADR candidates not selected by the above screening strategy (eg, ADR candidates with a very low occurrence rate, or single cases, or reported in SERENA-1 SERENA-2, China and Japan Phase I studies only). These were added to the list of ADRs if deemed medically relevant and suspected to be treatment related.

The common serious adverse reactions (≥ 1%) reported in patients receiving Etcamah in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor included infections (3.4%) and pyrexia (1.1%).

## 5.4.4. Adverse events of special interest, serious adverse events and deaths, other significant events

## 5.4.4.1. Bradycardia

This AESI was evaluated by a review of grouped PTs, comprising bradycardia and sinus bradycardia.

Based on the non-clinical toxicology and clinical safety findings observed across the camizestrant clinical program, bradycardia was an identified risk for camizestrant and classified as an AESI for further risk characterisation in the SERENA-6 study.

Timepoint Change from Baseline

Among the 19.8% patients with bradycardia reported, 85% patients had bradycardia reported as asymptomatic CTCAE Grade 1, while the remaining 15% had Grade 2 adverse reactions reported. Bradycardia exhibited a time-dependent onset where a gradual decrease in heart rate can reach a stable nadir at approximately 14 days and generally recovered following treatment cessation in a similar time course.

QTc prolongation has been reported as an adverse reaction in 18 patients (6.8%) within the Etcamah and CDK4/6 inhibitor combination. Of those, 14 were reported as Grade 1 or Grade 2; 3 were reported as Grade 3 and one as Grade 4 (all Grade 3 and 4 were in combination with ribociclib). There was one report of CTCAE Grade 4 QT prolongation for camizestrant in combination with ribociclib in a phase 1 study where ribociclib and camizestrant were administered concomitantly and ribociclib dose modification guidance was not followed. The case was also confounded by multiple contributing factors and the patient recovered.

## 5.4.4.2. Visual effects

This AESI was evaluated by a review of grouped PTs, comprising photopsia, vision blurred, visual impairment, diplopia, photophobia, visual perseveration, and glare.

Of the 40.7% patients who reported visual effects, the majority (92.5%) experienced CTCAE Grade 1 adverse reactions, while 6.5% patients experienced CTCAE Grade 2 adverse reactions and 0.9% CTCAE Grade 3 adverse reaction (photopsia) occurred. The most common visual effect was photopsia (24.0%). The median time to the onset of the first visual effect was 9 days. Visual effects ADRs were typically intermittent, of brief duration, and not continuous throughout the day (typically reported in the evening or morning). Visual effects were reported not to worsen over time in patients. Ophthalmological review of patients reporting visual effects showed no evidence of structural effects on the eye or clinically meaningful changes in visual acuity attributed to camizestrant.

At DC03, the adverse reactions leading to treatment discontinuation of Etcamah in patients receiving camizestrant in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor were neutropenia (0.4%) and electrocardiogram QT prolonged (0.4%).

## 5.4.7. Adverse events leading to dose modifications

The CSP guidance on dose modifications differed between the SERENA-1, SERENA-2, and SERENA-6 studies; and therefore, the dose modifications between SERENA-6 and the pooled data, including the monotherapy pool, were not comparable. At DCO3, the most common adverse reactions (≥ 2%) leading to dose modification of camizestrant in patients receiving camizestrant in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor were infections (6.8%), neutropenia (6.5%), bradycardia (4.2%), electrocardiogram QT prolonged (3.4%), diarrhoea (2.3%), vomiting (2.3%) and visual effects (2.3%)

## 5.4.9. Immunological events

Not applicable.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.4.10. Safety related to drug-drug interactions and other interactions

Camizestrant is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4, and thus inducers of CYP3A4 can reduce exposure levels of camizestrant. Since ribociclib is a potent inhibitor of CYP3A4, camizestrant exposure is increased approximately two-fold when administered together with ribociclib 400 mg and three-fold when coadministered with robiciclib 600 mg, which corresponds to the exposure reached with camizestrant 150 mg QD monotherapy.

Camizestrant is an inhibitor of CYP2B6, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19.
